<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

$2 \dfrac{3}{5} + 1 \dfrac{1}{2}$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 2 \ \dfrac {3} {5} + 1 \ \dfrac {1} {2} \\\\
&= \dfrac {13} {5} + \dfrac {3} {2} \\\\
&= \dfrac {13 \times 2} {5 \times 2} + \dfrac {3 \times 5} {2 \times 5} \\\\
&= \dfrac {26} {10} + \dfrac {15} {10} \\\\
&= \dfrac {26 + 15} {10} \\\\
&= \dfrac {41} {10} \\\\
&= 4 \ \dfrac {1} {10} 
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$4 \ \dfrac {1} {10}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
&= 2 \ \dfrac {3} {5} + 1 \ \dfrac {1} {2} \\\\
&= \dfrac {13} {5} + \dfrac {3} {2} \\\\
&= \dfrac {13 \times 2} {5 \times 2} + \dfrac {3 \times 5} {2 \times 5} \\\\
&= \dfrac {26} {10} + \dfrac {15} {10} \\\\
&= \dfrac {26 + 15} {10} \\\\
&= \dfrac {41} {10} \\\\
&= 4 \ \dfrac {1} {10} 
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$4 \ \dfrac {1} {10}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

What is the total surface area of all $6$ sides?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Surface area of cuboid} = 2 \times (\text{length} \times \text{width} + \text{width} \times \text {height} + \text {height} \times \text{length})$


As per the diagram,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Total surface area} &= 2 \times (8 \times 5 + 8 \times 6 + 6 \times 5) \\\\
                           &= 2 \times (40 + 48 + 30) \\\\
                           &= 2 \times 118 \\\\
                           &= 236 \text {cm}^2
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$236 \text {cm}^2$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_katex_formula_surface_area_cuboid

As per the diagram,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Total surface area} &= 2 \times (8 \times 5 + 8 \times 6 + 6 \times 5) \\\\
                           &= 2 \times (40 + 48 + 30) \\\\
                           &= 2 \times 118 \\\\
                           &= 236 \text {cm}^2
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

If the area of the front face doubles but the $8 \ \text{cm}$ width does not change what must the new height be?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Area of rectangle} = \text{length} \times \text{width}$


Let the new height $= h$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
h \times 8      &= 2 \times (6 \times 8) \\\\
8h              &= 2 \times 6 \times 8 \\\\
h               &= \dfrac {2 \times 6 \times 8} {8} \\\\
h               &= \dfrac {2 \times 6 \times \cancel {8}} {\cancel {8}} \\\\
h               &= 2 \times 6 \\\\
h               &= 12 \ \text {cm}
\end{aligned}
$

The new height is $12 \ \text {cm}$.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$12 \ \text {cm}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_katex_formula_area_rectangle_expanded

Let the new height $= h$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
h \times 8      &= 2 \times (6 \times 8) \\\\
8h              &= 2 \times 6 \times 8 \\\\
h               &= \dfrac {2 \times 6 \times 8} {8} \\\\
h               &= \dfrac {2 \times 6 \times \cancel {8}} {\cancel {8}} \\\\
h               &= 2 \times 6 \\\\
h               &= 12 \ \text {cm}
\end{aligned}
$

The new height is $12 \ \text {cm}$.
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$12 \ \text {cm}$
</code></pre>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-632B3C63-276D-4B09-BFCB-80AB16809792'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

The distance between Richmond and Kingston is approximately $3 \dfrac{3}{8} \ \text{miles}$. If I walk $\dfrac{2}{3}$ of the way, 
how far do I walk?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Distance travelled} &= \dfrac {2} {3} \ \text {of} \ 3 \dfrac {3} {8} \\\\
                           &= \dfrac {2} {3} \times \dfrac {27} {8} \\\\
                           &= \dfrac {2 \times 27} {3 \times 8} \\\\
                           &= \dfrac {2 \times 3 \times 9} {3 \times 2 \times 4} \\\\
                           &= \dfrac {\cancel {2} \times \cancel {3} \times 9} {\cancel {3} \times \cancel {2} \times 4} \\\\
                           &= \dfrac {9} {4} \ \text {miles}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\dfrac {9} {4} \ \text {miles}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Distance travelled} &= \dfrac {2} {3} \ \text {of} \ 3 \dfrac {3} {8} \\\\
                           &= \dfrac {2} {3} \times \dfrac {27} {8} \\\\
                           &= \dfrac {2 \times 27} {3 \times 8} \\\\
                           &= \dfrac {2 \times 3 \times 9} {3 \times 2 \times 4} \\\\
                           &= \dfrac {\cancel {2} \times \cancel {3} \times 9} {\cancel {3} \times \cancel {2} \times 4} \\\\
                           &= \dfrac {9} {4} \ \text {miles}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\dfrac {9} {4} \ \text {miles}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-33894696-6D5A-4AA2-89AF-C94F80BC4363'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Three bicycles are priced at $\pounds 79, \pounds 80$ and $\pounds 84$. If the shopkeeper decided to sell them for the same total amount, but made the three prices equal, what would each cost?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>


As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Cost of each bicycle}          &= \dfrac {79 + 80 + 84 } {3} \\\\
                                      &= \dfrac {243} {3} \\\\
                                      &= \dfrac {81 \times 3} {3} \\\\
                                      &= \dfrac {81 \times \cancel 3} {\cancel 3} \\\\
                                      &= \pounds 81
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 81$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>
As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Cost of each bicycle}          &= \dfrac {79 + 80 + 84 } {3} \\\\
                                      &= \dfrac {243} {3} \\\\
                                      &= \dfrac {81 \times 3} {3} \\\\
                                      &= \dfrac {81 \times \cancel 3} {\cancel 3} \\\\
                                      &= \pounds 81
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\pounds 81$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

</div>
<div class='workings'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {He was wrong by} &= 3.51 - 3.15 \\\\
                        &= 0.36
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$0.36$

</div>
</div>

\begin{aligned}
\text {He was wrong by} &= 3.51 - 3.15 \\\\
                        &= 0.36
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-370E6891-00C4-4400-9412-56FFA4BE4110'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

A car hire firm charges $\pounds 35$ per day plus $34 \text{p}$ per km travelled. A man hired a car for $4$ days and travelled 
$300 \ \text{km}$. How much did he pay?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Amount he paid}                &= 4 \times \pounds 35 + 300 \times 34 \text {p} \\\\
                                      &= \pounds 140 + 10200 \text {p} \\\\
                                      &= \pounds 140 + \pounds \dfrac {10200} {100} \\\\
                                      &= 140 +  \dfrac {102 \times 100} {100} \\\\
                                      &= 140 +  \dfrac {102 \times \cancel {100}} { \cancel {100}} \\\\
                                      &= 140 +  102 \\\\
                                      &= \pounds 242
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 242$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Amount he paid}                &= 4 \times \pounds 35 + 300 \times 34 \text {p} \\\\
                                      &= \pounds 140 + 10200 \text {p} \\\\
                                      &= \pounds 140 + \pounds \dfrac {10200} {100} \\\\
                                      &= 140 +  \dfrac {102 \times 100} {100} \\\\
                                      &= 140 +  \dfrac {102 \times \cancel {100}} { \cancel {100}} \\\\
                                      &= 140 +  102 \\\\
                                      &= \pounds 242
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\pounds 242$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-66B16F07-8FF6-4E55-BA74-5529DF432D2B'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

On $1 \text{st}$ January $2006$ my grandmother was $80$ years old. Her daughter was $40$ years old on $1 \ \text{st}$ January $1994$. 
How old was my grandmother when her daughter was born?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Grandmother's age in 2006}   &= 80 \\\\
\text {Daughter's age in 2006}      &= 40 + (2006 - 1994) \\\\
                                    &= 40 + 12 \\\\
                                    &= 52 \ \text {years} \\\\
\text{Difference}                   &= 80 - 52 \\\\
                                    &= 28 \ \text {years}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$28 \ \text {years}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Grandmother's age in 2006}   &= 80 \\\\
\text {Daughter's age in 2006}      &= 40 + (2006 - 1994) \\\\
                                    &= 40 + 12 \\\\
                                    &= 52 \ \text {years} \\\\
\text{Difference}                   &= 80 - 52 \\\\
                                    &= 28 \ \text {years}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$28 \ \text {years}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Sam thinks of a number, multiplies it by $2$ and then adds $11$. The answer he gets is $29$. What number did he think of?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a \times 2 + 11 &= 29 \\\\
a \times 2      &= 29 - 11 \\\\
a \times 2      &= 18 \\\\
a               &= \dfrac {18} {2} \\\\
a               &= \dfrac {9 \times \cancel {2}} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
a               &= 9
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$9$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a \times 2 + 11 &= 29 \\\\
a \times 2      &= 29 - 11 \\\\
a \times 2      &= 18 \\\\
a               &= \dfrac {18} {2} \\\\
a               &= \dfrac {9 \times \cancel {2}} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
a               &= 9
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$9$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Julia thinks of a number, multiplies it by $5$, adds $11$ and then divides it by $2$. She gets the answer $28$. 
What number did she think of?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
(a \times 5 + 11) \div 2 &= 28 \\\\
a \times 5 + 11          &= 28 \times 2 \\\\
a \times 5 + 11          &= 56 \\\\
a \times 5               &= 56 - 11 \\\\
a \times 5               &= 45 \\\\
a                        &= \dfrac {45} {5} \\\\
a                        &= \dfrac {9 \times 5} {5} \\\\
a                        &= \dfrac {9 \times \cancel {5}} {\cancel {5}} \\\\
a                        &= 9
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$9$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
(a \times 5 + 11) \div 2 &= 28 \\\\
a \times 5 + 11          &= 28 \times 2 \\\\
a \times 5 + 11          &= 56 \\\\
a \times 5               &= 56 - 11 \\\\
a \times 5               &= 45 \\\\
a                        &= \dfrac {45} {5} \\\\
a                        &= \dfrac {9 \times 5} {5} \\\\
a                        &= \dfrac {9 \times \cancel {5}} {\cancel {5}} \\\\
a                        &= 9
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$9$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Sam and Julia find there is one number for which, if they both start from that number and follow their own rules, they get the same answer. What is the number they both start with?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number they both start with $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a \times 2 + 11     &= (a \times 5 + 11) \div 2 \\\\
(2 a + 11) \times 2 &= 5 a + 11 \\\\
4 a + 22            &= 5 a + 11 \\\\
4 a - 5 a           &= 11 - 22 \\\\
-a                  &= -11 \\\\
\cancel {-} a       &= \cancel {-} 11 \\\\
a                   &= 11
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$11$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number they both start with $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a \times 2 + 11     &= (a \times 5 + 11) \div 2 \\\\
(2 a + 11) \times 2 &= 5 a + 11 \\\\
4 a + 22            &= 5 a + 11 \\\\
4 a - 5 a           &= 11 - 22 \\\\
-a                  &= -11 \\\\
\cancel {-} a       &= \cancel {-} 11 \\\\
a                   &= 11
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$11$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-1F0FD520-2214-45CF-BFA5-2CDD7CBDAADE'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

A lady has some dogs and some kennels for them to sleep in.

If she puts $3$ dogs in each kennel, there are $2$ dogs left over. 

If she puts $4$ dogs in each kennel, $1$ kennel is left empty.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

TODOWORKING

</div>
<div class='working'>

TODOWORKING

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

TODOANSWER

</div>
<div class='answer'>

TODOANSWER

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>TODOWORKING
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>TODOWORKING
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>

</div>

</div>

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How many dogs are there ?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let number of dogs $= a$

Let number of kennels $= b$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a &= 3b + 2 \\\\
a &= 4(b - 1)                           
\end{aligned}
$

Let's substitute a from the first equation to reduce it to just one variable.

$
\begin{aligned}
4(b - 1) &= 3b + 2 \\\\
4b - 4   &= 3b + 2 \\\\  
4b - 3b  &= 2 + 4 \\\\
b        &= 6 \\\\
a        &= 3b + 2 \\\\
a        &= 3 \times 6 + 2 \\\\
a        &= 18 + 2 \\\\
a        &= 20                       
\end{aligned}
$

Number of dogs $= 20$


</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$20$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let number of dogs $= a$

$
\begin{aligned}

4b - 4   &= 3b + 2 \\\\  
4b - 3b  &= 2 + 4 \\\\
b        &= 6 \\\\
a        &= 3b + 2 \\\\
a        &= 3 \times 6 + 2 \\\\
a        &= 18 + 2 \\\\
a        &= 20                       
\end{aligned}
$

Number of dogs $= 20$

</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</code></pre>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-E9A0FA5F-BD86-427B-91F1-7D0A086BB6AE'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

$\Psi$ is a new mathematical rule which works like this:

To work out $2 \ \Psi\  4$ add $1$ to $2$, then multiply by $4$, like this:

$2 + 1 = 3$

$3 \times 4 = 12$

So $2 \ \Psi\  4 = 12$

Similarly $4 \ \Psi\  7 = 35$ because $4 + 1 = 5$, then $5 \times 7 = 35$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

%empty%

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

%empty%

</div>
</div>

</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question subquestion'>

Work out $3 \ \Psi\  8$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 3 \ \Psi \ 8 \\\\
&= (3 + 1) \times 8 \\\\
&= 4 \times 8 \\\\
&= 32
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

</div>

<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

If $2 \ \Psi\  y = 18$, what number does $y$ stand for?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
2 \ \Psi \ y     &= 18 \\\\
(2 + 1) \times y &= 18 \\\\
3 \times y       &= 18 \\\\
y                &= \dfrac {18} {3} \\\\
y                &= \dfrac {6 \times 3} {3} \\\\
y                &= \dfrac {6 \times \cancel {3}} {\cancel {3}} \\\\
y                &= 6
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$6$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
2 \ \Psi \ y     &= 18 \\\\
(2 + 1) \times y &= 18 \\\\
3 \times y       &= 18 \\\\
y                &= \dfrac {18} {3} \\\\
y                &= \dfrac {6 \times 3} {3} \\\\
y                &= \dfrac {6 \times \cancel {3}} {\cancel {3}} \\\\
y                &= 6
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$6$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

If $z \ \Psi\  4 = 36$, what number does $z$ stand for?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
z \ \Psi \ 4     &= 36 \\\\
(z + 1) \times 4 &= 36 \\\\
z + 1            &= \dfrac {36} {4} \\\\
z + 1            &= \dfrac {9 \times 4} {4} \\\\
z + 1            &= \dfrac {9 \times \cancel {4}} {\cancel {4}} \\\\
z + 1            &= 9 \\\\
z                &= 9 - 1 \\\\
z                &= 8
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$8$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
z \ \Psi \ 4     &= 36 \\\\
(z + 1) \times 4 &= 36 \\\\
z + 1            &= \dfrac {36} {4} \\\\
z + 1            &= \dfrac {9 \times 4} {4} \\\\
z + 1            &= \dfrac {9 \times \cancel {4}} {\cancel {4}} \\\\
z + 1            &= 9 \\\\
z                &= 9 - 1 \\\\
z                &= 8
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$8$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>

<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

If $p \ \Psi\  q = 20$, find all the pairs of whole numbers that $p$ and $q$ could be.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
p \ \Psi \ q     &= 20 \\\\
(p + 1) \times q &= 20
\end{aligned}
$

Possible values $=$

$
\begin{aligned}
(p + 1) &= 4 \quad \text{and} \quad q = 5 \\\\
p       &= 3 \quad \text{and} \quad q = 5
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
(p + 1) &= 5 \quad \text{and} \quad q = 4 \\\\
p       &= 4 \quad \text{and} \quad q = 4
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
(p + 1) &= 10 \quad \text{and} \quad q = 2 \\\\
p       &= 9 \quad \text{and} \quad q = 2
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
(p + 1) &= 2 \quad \text{and} \quad q = 10 \\\\
p       &= 1 \quad \text{and} \quad q = 10
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
(p + 1) &= 20 \quad \text{and} \quad q = 1 \\\\
p       &= 19 \quad \text{and} \quad q = 1
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
(p + 1) &= 1 \quad \text{and} \quad q = 20 \\\\
p       &= 0 \quad \text{and} \quad q = 20
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$(3,5), \ (4,4), \ (9,2), \ (1,10), \ (19,1), \ (0,20)$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

If a bag of $2 \ \text{pence}$ coins weighs $350 \ \text{g}$, how many coins are there?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of coins} &= \dfrac {350} {7} \\\\
                       &= \dfrac {50 \times 7} {7} \\\\
                       &= \dfrac {50 \times \cancel {7}} {\cancel {7}} \\\\
                       &= 50
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$50$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of coins} &= \dfrac {350} {7} \\\\
                       &= \dfrac {50 \times 7} {7} \\\\
                       &= \dfrac {50 \times \cancel {7}} {\cancel {7}} \\\\
                       &= 50
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How much are $500 \ \text{g}$ of $20 \ \text{pence}$ coins worth?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of coins} &= \dfrac {500} {5} \\\\
                       &= \dfrac {5 \times 100} {5} \\\\
                       &= \dfrac {\cancel {5} \times 100} {\cancel {5}} \\\\
                       &= 100
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Worth} &= 100 \times 20 \text{p} \\\\
             &= \pounds \dfrac {2000} {100} \\\\
             &= \dfrac {20 \cancel {0} \cancel {0}} {1 \cancel {0} \cancel {0}} \\\\
             &= \pounds 20
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 20$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

\end{aligned}

             &= \pounds \dfrac {2000} {100} \\\\
             &= \dfrac {20 \cancel {0} \cancel {0}} {1 \cancel {0} \cancel {0}} \\\\
             &= \pounds 20
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How many grams would $\pounds 10$ worth of $50 \ \text{pence}$ coins weigh?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of coins} &= \dfrac {10 \times 100} {50} \\\\
                       &= \dfrac {5 \times 2 \times 100} {5 \times 10} \\\\
                       &= \dfrac {\cancel {5} \times 2 \times 10 \cancel {0}} {\cancel {5} \times 1 \cancel {0}} \\\\
                       &= 20
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Total weight} &= 20 \times 13.4 \ \text{g} \\\\
                    &= 268 \ \text{g}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>

$268 \ \text{g}$

                       &= 20
\end{aligned}
$

$

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-0FE51052-D2CD-4586-8EBB-DB99CD3025CA'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

On an Autumn day the number of leaves on a tree at the end of the day is half the number at the start of the day. 
A tree initially has $400000$ leaves. 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

%empty%

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

%empty%

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>

</div>

</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How many are left at the end of the second day?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Leaves left at the end of second day} &= \dfrac {1} {2} \times \dfrac {1} {2} \times 400000 \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {400000} {4} \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {100000 \times 4} {4} \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {100000 \times \cancel {4}} {\cancel {4}} \\\\
                                            &= 100000
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$100000$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Leaves left at the end of second day} &= \dfrac {1} {2} \times \dfrac {1} {2} \times 400000 \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {400000} {4} \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {100000 \times 4} {4} \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {100000 \times \cancel {4}} {\cancel {4}} \\\\
                                            &= 100000
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$100000$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

At the end of which day are there less than $2000$ leaves?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the day be $n$th day

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Day on which there are less than 2000 leaves} &= \dfrac {400000} {2^n} \\\\
                                                    &= \dfrac {2000 \times 200} {2^n}
\end{aligned}
$

Since $2^8 = 256$

Hence $n = 8$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$8$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the day be $n$th day

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Day on which there are less than 2000 leaves} &= \dfrac {400000} {2^n} \\\\
                                                    &= \dfrac {2000 \times 200} {2^n}
\end{aligned}
$

Since $2^8 = 256$

Hence $n = 8$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$8$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-4113B329-3FF2-4CCB-9547-DFD49DC41DC7'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

A perfect number is one in which all its factors (except the number itself) total to the number. 
For example $6 = 1 + 2 + 3$ is a perfect number since the proper factors of $6$ are $1, 2$ and $3$ and they total to $6$. 
$12$ is not a perfect number as its proper factors are $1, 2, 3, 4, 6$ and they total $16$. 

</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-8F027A1E-78A4-45E0-BB41-9FAAB4D6B88A'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

In a barn there are only horses and people. If there are $12$ heads and $34$ legs how many horses and how many people are there?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let number of people $= a$

Let number of horses $= b$

As per the question,

$
\begin {aligned}
a + b   &= 12 \\\\
2a + 4b &= 34
\end {aligned}
$

Let's substitute b from the first equation to reduce it to just one variable.

$
\begin {aligned}
2(12 - b) + 4b        &= 34 \\\\
2 \times 12 - 2b + 4b &= 34 \\\\
24 + 2b               &= 34 \\\\
2b                    &= 34 - 24 \\\\
2b                    &= 10 \\\\
b                     &= \dfrac {10} {2} \\\\
b                     &= \dfrac {5 \times 2} {2} \\\\
b                     &= \dfrac {5 \times \cancel 2} {\cancel 2} \\\\
b                     &= 5 \\\\
a                     &= 12 - b \\\\
a                     &= 12 - 5 \\\\
a                     &= 7
\end {aligned}
$

Number of people $= 7$

Number of horses $= 5$



</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

Number of people $=$ $7$

</div>
<div class='answer'>

Number of horses $=$ $12$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let number of people $= a$

Let number of horses $= b$

As per the question,

$
\begin {aligned}
a + b   &= 12 \\\\
2a + 4b &= 34
\end {aligned}
$

Let's substitute b from the first equation to reduce it to just one variable.

$
\begin {aligned}
2(12 - b) + 4b        &= 34 \\\\
2 \times 12 - 2b + 4b &= 34 \\\\
24 + 2b               &= 34 \\\\
2b                    &= 34 - 24 \\\\
2b                    &= 10 \\\\
b                     &= \dfrac {10} {2} \\\\
b                     &= \dfrac {5 \times 2} {2} \\\\
b                     &= \dfrac {5 \times \cancel 2} {\cancel 2} \\\\
b                     &= 5 \\\\
a                     &= 12 - b \\\\
a                     &= 12 - 5 \\\\
a                     &= 7
\end {aligned}
$

Number of people $= 7$

Number of horses $= 5$


</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Number of people</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$7$
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Number of horses</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$12$
</code></pre>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-9332B2DF-AC30-43E8-91FA-BFE3FFFBDE8A'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

How many $3 \ \text{cm}$ cubes can be made by cutting up a block $12 \ \text{cm}$ by $24 \ \text{cm}$ by $18 \ \text{cm}$?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of cubes} &= \dfrac {\text{Volume of cuboid}} {\text{Volume of cube}} \\\\
                       &= \dfrac {12 \times 24 \times 18} {3 \times 3 \times 3} \\\\
                       &= \dfrac {3 \times 4 \times 3 \times 8 \times 6 \times 3} {3 \times 3 \times 3} \\\\
                       &= \dfrac {\cancel {3} \times 4 \times \cancel {3} \times 8 \times 6 \times \cancel{3}} {\cancel {3} \times \cancel {3} \times \cancel {3}} \\\\
                       &= 4 \times 8 \times 6 \\\\
                       &= 32 \times 6 \\\\
                       &= 192
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$192$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of cubes} &= \dfrac {\text{Volume of cuboid}} {\text{Volume of cube}} \\\\
                       &= \dfrac {12 \times 24 \times 18} {3 \times 3 \times 3} \\\\
                       &= \dfrac {3 \times 4 \times 3 \times 8 \times 6 \times 3} {3 \times 3 \times 3} \\\\
                       &= \dfrac {\cancel {3} \times 4 \times \cancel {3} \times 8 \times 6 \times \cancel{3}} {\cancel {3} \times \cancel {3} \times \cancel {3}} \\\\
                       &= 4 \times 8 \times 6 \\\\
                       &= 32 \times 6 \\\\
                       &= 192
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$192$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-8C3738E6-75D6-4E8F-912D-0AA1EEB6F854'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Find angles $b, x$ and $y$ in the following diagrams (not to scale):

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)


</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

*ABBR: TODO:: Add drawing starts.*

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)



*TODO:: Add the second figure with vertices marked ABCD. The bigger triangle should be ABC and smaller one ADC.*


*ABBR: TODO:: Add drawing ends.*


ABBR: $\text{Angles on a straight line add up to} = 180 \degree$.


ABBR: $\text {Alternate interior angles are equal when two parallel lines are cut by a transversal}$.

As per the first diagram,

$
\begin{aligned}
b + b + 108     &= 180 \\\\
2b + 108        &= 180 \\\\
2b              &= 180 - 108 \\\\
2b              &= 72 \\\\
b               &= \dfrac {72} {2} \\\\
b               &= \dfrac {36 \times 2} {2} \\\\
b               &= \dfrac {36 \times \cancel {2}} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
b               &= 36 \degree
\end{aligned}
$

Let the third angle in triangle ABC $= a$

As per the diagram,

$
\begin{aligned}
a + 48 + 34 + 53        &= 180 \\\\
a + 135                 &= 180 \\\\
a                       &= 180 - 135 \\\\
a                       &= 45 \degree
\end{aligned}
$

Let's use the value of $a$ to find $x$.

$
\begin{aligned}
x + x + a               &= 180 \\\\
2x + 45                 &= 180 \\\\
2x                      &= 180 - 45 \\\\
2x                      &= 135 \\\\
x                       &= \dfrac {135} {2} \\\\
x                       &= 67.5 \degree
\end{aligned}
$

Let's use the value of $x$ and $a$ to find $y$.

As per the diagram, in triangle ADC

$
\begin{aligned}
x + a + y + 34               &= 180 \\\\
67.5 + 45 + y + 34           &= 180 \\\\
y + 146.5                    &= 180 \\\\
y                            &= 180 - 146.5 \\\\
y                            &= 33.5 \degree
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$
\begin{aligned}
b &= 36 \degree \\\\
x &= 67.5 \degree \\\\
y &= 33.5 \degree \\\\
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
